# Aegis PMA brief: for Thu 5 Nov 2026 (AQE data to the Wed 4 Nov close)

Session Thu 5 Nov SGT, AQE run started 06:25 and exported 06:44 SGT on the Wed 4 Nov close; 46 names in the room, slate cap 25, 12 survivors of the evidence round, none left off by the cap. Ten ADVANCE, one HOLD-FOR-CONDITIONS (KDP), one dropped on objections (USFD), 34 not slated; 1R is $620 from the fund file, reported and not changed.

## §0A EXECUTIVE CONSENSUS: WHY THESE ADVANCE

- **What the ten share:** every one is on AQE's Longlist, every one sits within 1% of an hourly breakout line (0.11% to 0.93% under it), seven have a 30-day beta of 0.15 or lower, and six are Staples, Healthcare or Financials names, so this is a defensive-leaning breakout list in a broadening tape.
- **Where agreement is strongest and thinnest:** the whole room backed KO; ABBV, CAH, GLBE, MFC and CCEP were near-unanimous; BBY, DG and BALL were carried over real objections; WCN is the narrowest.
- **What kills the group thesis:** breakouts being sold in chop, which would show first as SPY losing the 750 put wall and the staples names closing back under their breakout levels together (KO 95.37, CCEP 114.55, DG 133.79).

### KO: ADVANCE · committee conviction 3
A market leader at a new high (0.03% under its 52-week high) on 1.61x volume, gaining on down days with every average stacked in order, made the lead name of the beverage group. Nothing was argued against the name itself; the losing point was only that its relative-strength rank (76.39) sits at the low end of leadership and that KO and CCEP are one beverage bet. It has to keep closing above 95.37, the breakout level.

### ABBV: ADVANCE · committee conviction 3
A leader that pulled back to its breakout, held it on 1.56x volume and closed near the top of the day, relative-strength rank 88.92, with CAH confirming healthcare. The losing argument: the daily trend has only one strong bar after a failed leg, so the trigger acts on a fragment and should wait for a second strong daily close. It must hold the 283.32 test low.

### CAH: ADVANCE · committee conviction 4
The best base in the room: a new high at its 52-week high, closing at the top of the day on 1.44x volume, relative-strength rank 86.51, with ABBV confirming healthcare. The losing argument: the daily trend is choppy and its money-flow score (44.7) is the weakest of the ten, so the break may lack accumulation. It ranks 279th of 395 on AQE's own list, the lowest of the ten.

### GLBE: ADVANCE · committee conviction 4
A new high closing at 99.6% of the day's range on 1.54x volume, relative-strength rank 89.16, every average in order, with fast earnings growth behind it. The losing argument: at 13.31% above its 20-day it is away from value, and an hourly close above yesterday's high buys the extension rather than a test. Earnings on 18 Nov fall inside the hold.

### MFC: ADVANCE · committee conviction 3
A fresh break to a new high on 1.63x volume, the day after it dipped under its rising trendline (45.05) and closed back above it, every average in order. The losing argument: the daily trend has one strong bar after a run of middling ones, and its relative-strength rank (70.36) sits on the leadership floor. It sits in Financials, where the macro read is caution and the book already holds V.

### CCEP: ADVANCE · committee conviction 3
A beverage bottler at a new high on 1.40x volume that has beaten SPY by 16.19% over 20 days, more than KO, with every average in order. The losing argument: the last hourly candle closed red at 54.7% of its range, so supply met the break and volume only matched the 1.4x floor; the case was to wait for a stronger bar. It moves with KO and DG as one staples bet.

### BBY: ADVANCE · committee conviction 4
The strongest relative strength of the ten (rank 96.14) at a new high, closing at 97.2% of the hourly range after a one-day shakeout, every average in order. The losing argument, carried over and still unanswered on the ticket: breakout volume was 1.31x, under the 1.4x bar, which makes it a rejection rather than a weaker buy, and a bearish divergence sits at the high. Earnings on 24 Nov fall inside the hold.

### DG: ADVANCE · committee conviction 4
A discount retailer that gains on down days broke through its structure on a wide bar at 1.68x volume and closed at 96.7% of the hourly range. The losing arguments: the 100-day average (123.87) is still under the 200-day (125.57), so the long-term trend is not confirmed, and the buy line sits at the top of its price channel rather than at value. Earnings on 3 Dec sit at the edge of the 20-session hold.

### BALL: ADVANCE · committee conviction 3
A first exit from a long sideways base 1.01% under its high on 1.6x volume, closing at 97.4% of the hourly range, with a weekly trend of higher highs. The losing argument: the 50-day (61.27) is under the 100-day (61.75) and relative-strength rank is 68.25, under the leadership floor, so this is a base resolving, not a confirmed uptrend. It is a young trend and the room rated it accordingly.

### WCN: ADVANCE · committee conviction 2
A new high on 2.11x volume with higher highs on the weekly, daily and hourly charts; strength itself is the signal. The losing arguments: it is 16.56% above its 20-day and 4.14 daily ranges above value, so an hourly breakout buy is a chase, and its daily trend reading is a plateau with no fresh entry signal. In the room on a reserved reclaim seat (no voice nominated it), it is the narrowest advance and the only one outside a leading sector.

## §0B LANE NOTE

KO, GLBE, CCEP, BBY and DG (and the KDP hold and the dropped USFD) are MOMENTUM-lane verdicts, decided by the strength voices; ABBV, CAH, MFC, BALL and WCN are PULLBACK-lane verdicts, decided by the whole room. All ten tickets carry the same entry, an hourly close above yesterday's high, so the lane changed who decided, not how the name is bought.

- Nine of ten advances sit in leading, gate-PASS groups except MFC (Financials gate WATCH) and WCN (Industrials, a HOLD sector); the uncomfortable version is that the list is buying into groups already up 10% to 12.5% in a month, late in the move by AQE's own label, and the one held name shares MFC's caution-graded sector.
